  WHY THIS MATTERS (context)
  This is roadmap step 1 toward multi-agent collaboration. Entire's brain is durable
  agent memory
  (sessions + seed + semantic + durable facts). Today it's safe for ONE writer; the
  vision needs
  FLEETS of agents reasoning and writing together. Before any of that, the local brain
  must be safe
  for N concurrent writers on one machine — otherwise two agents writing at once
  corrupt or lose
  each other's work. This is the prerequisite for everything downstream
  (shared/distributed brain,
  agent-to-agent coordination). A recent audit already flagged the concrete gap: the
  watch daemon
  has a TOCTOU race on its cursor (no lock), so two watchers double-spend the distill
  budget.

  REPO
  entire-brain (this checkout, github ashtom/entire-brain). Go. Harness/tests via `mise
  run check`
  (gofmt, go vet, go test -race, cross-builds mac/linux/windows).

  GOAL
  Make every brain WRITE path concurrency-safe: serialized with a cross-platform file
  lock,
  atomic on disk, and with a defined merge so simultaneous writers never clobber, lose,
  or
  duplicate data. Preserve restart-safety and the no-egress guarantee.

  - internal/cli/watch.go — watchCursor, loadWatchCursor/saveWatchCursor,
  writeFileAtomic,
    watchShouldDistill + the budget/LastDistillAt logic (the known TOCTOU:
  read-modify-write of the
    cursor is not atomic across processes).

  (semantic.go);

  - env.go / path.go for the brain dir layout (where locks should live, per repo-key +
  branch).
  - go.mod — check whether a flock-style dep is already vendored before adding one.

  SCOPE (do all four):
  1. ONE reusable file-lock primitive for brain writes — cross-platform
  (mac/linux/windows). Prefer
     an existing vendored dep or a small lockfile abstraction (O_CREATE|O_EXCL with
  stale-lock
     handling, or syscall flock + LockFileEx behind build tags). Bounded acquisition
  with a clear,
     already-styled error (like `index_locked`) on contention/timeout — fail fast,
  don't hang.
  2. Make cursor + facts writes READ-MODIFY-WRITE UNDER THE LOCK. Fix the watch TOCTOU:
  acquire the
     lock, re-read the cursor, re-check budget/interval, write, release — so two
  watchers can't both
     pass the gate and double-spend. Same for facts/manifest appends.
  3. ATOMIC writes everywhere — ensure all brain writes go through writeFileAtomic
  (temp file +
     rename), not partial in-place writes. Audit every brain write for this.
  4. DEFINED MERGE for concurrent fact writes — when two writers add facts at once, the
  result must
     be deterministic and lossless: dedup by content-id, provenance-ordered,
  branch-scoped, no lost
     appends. Reuse the existing reconcile semantics; don't fork them.

  HARD RULES
  - No network, no egress; local-only. Inspectable on-disk artifacts unchanged in
  format.
  - No behavior change for the single-writer case (the common path stays fast; lock is
  uncontended).
  - Cross-platform: must pass on windows (locking is the dangerous part there).
  - Restart-safety preserved (atomic + lock must not leave stale locks that wedge a
  fresh process —
    handle stale-lock recovery).
  - TESTS ARE THE POINT: add a concurrency test that spawns N goroutines (and ideally N
  processes)
    hammering the same cursor + facts file, and asserts: no corruption, no lost writes,
  no
    duplicated facts, and the budget cursor is never double-spent (the original bug).
  Must pass
    under `go test -race`.

  WORKFLOW
  1. Produce a PLAN: the list of every brain write path you found (file:line), the lock
  primitive
     you'll use and why, where the lock files live, the merge policy for concurrent
  facts, and the
     concurrency test design. STOP for approval.
  2. Then implement smallest-diff-first: lock primitive → watch cursor (fix the known
  TOCTOU) →

  cross-build) and
     report green. Honest commit messages; don't claim multi-agent collaboration is
  "done" — this

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Local Brain Write Concurrency Safety

## Summary
Make persistent brain writes serializable, atomic, and merge-safe with no network and no new module dependency. I’ll use `golang.org/x/sys`, already present in `go.mod`, for native advisory file locks: `unix.Flock` on macOS/Linux and `windows.LockFileEx` on Windows. This avoids stale lock wedging because the OS releases the lock when a process exits; the lock file may remain as metadata but won’t block restart.

Locks will live under the repo’s existing keyed store:
- Brain data writes: `<PluginDataDir>/repos/<repo-key>/locks/write.lock`
- Watch cursor writes: `<PluginStateDir>/repos/<repo-key>/locks/watch.lock`
- Use one reusable primitive with styled errors such as `brain_locked:` and existing semantic wrappers preserving `index_locked:` wording.

## Write Paths Found
Persistent brain/data writes to lock and/or audit:
- `internal/cli/watch.go:160,176,182,206,346` cursor read/write and gated spend TOCTOU.
- `internal/cli/brain.go:119,140,175,183,186,192` manifest/README read-modify-write and atomic helper.
- `internal/cli/export.go:277,301,307,316,321,1693,1698,1775,1781,1822,1853` sessions, transcript files, manifest, history index, export cursor, stale transcript cleanup.
- `internal/cli/seed.go:291,323,341,681,685,688,691,694,697,700,703,720,1073,1078,1373,1518,1530` seed artifacts, seed source manifest, seed cursor, agent artifacts.
- `internal/cli/history.go:151,161,170,181,320` history index, scan cache, manifest source update.
- `internal/cli/doc.go:123,134,142,153` doc index and manifest source update.
- `internal/cli/facts.go:224,240,358,368` facts NDJSON and taxonomy writes.
- `internal/cli/reconcile.go:51,55,73` proposal queue remove/write.
- `internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:199,244,407,421,425,428,435,453,673,682` distill cache, facts/proposals/taxonomy, manifest facts source.
- `internal/cli/facts_manifest.go:54,55,65,82` full fact manifest rebuilder.
- `internal/cli/facts_write_cmd.go:75,80,83,86` `remember` load/upsert/write/manifest.
- `internal/cli/facts_admin_cmd.go:45,55,58,117,121,168,171,174,239,243,249,253,254,258,298,317,320` retract/review/promote/gc fact mutations.
- `internal/cli/semantic.go:267,405,443,475,538,595,612,615,621,626,664,677,680,692,995,1042,1053,1060,1314,1354,1370,3713,4035,4203,4737,4824,4836,4953,4956,4989,5408` semantic index/repair/reset/import/export/audit writes. Existing semantic locking will be refactored onto the shared primitive.

Atomic-only/non-brain outputs noted but not central: eval/expansion caches and explicit user output files (`facts_eval.go:328`, `facts_expand.go:106`, `facts_eval_gen.go:81`) can be switched to `writeFileAtomic` opportunistically without changing brain formats.

## Key Changes
- Add a small internal lock abstraction with build-tagged implementations:
  - `filelock_unix.go`: open/create lock file, `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`, bounded retry, truncate/write metadata.
  - `filelock_windows.go`: open/create lock file, `LockFileEx` exclusive nonblocking, bounded retry, truncate/write metadata.
  - Shared wrapper returns clear timeout/contention errors and a `Close`/unlock function.
- Replace `acquireSemanticIndexLock` internals with the shared primitive while keeping `index_locked` error style. Semantic commands keep their existing behavior but gain Windows-safe locking and no stale O_EXCL wedge.
- Add `withBrainWriteLock(brainDir, fn)` and locked helper variants for manifest/source updates to avoid nested lock deadlocks.
- Use the repo-wide brain write lock for persistent manifest/source/session/seed/history/doc/facts/semantic write sequences. Explicit `--output` exports remain isolated but still use atomic writes.
- Fix watch TOCTOU by changing the tick to re-read and reserve under the watch cursor lock:
  - Compute fingerprint and run deterministic refresh as today.
  - Acquire watch lock, re-read cursor, re-check fingerprint/interval/budget.
  - Atomically save refreshed cursor and, when agent work is allowed, reserve `LastAgentSpendAt` before releasing the lock.
  - Run seed/distill only if this process won the reservation. This prevents double-spend without holding the lock through long agent calls.
- Define fact merge at commit time:
  - All fact mutations load current branch facts under lock.
  - Incoming records are folded with existing `upsertFact`, preserving content-id dedupe and `unionFactAnchors`.
  - Existing reconcile semantics stay authoritative for merge/supersede decisions; no new fact format or alternate reconciler.
  - Manifest facts source is rebuilt from disk while still holding the brain write lock.
- Atomic-write audit:
  - Convert direct brain `os.WriteFile` calls to `writeFileAtomic`, especially transcripts, seed markdown/docs, seed agent artifacts, JSON helper writes, imported semantic generation staging writes, and semantic audit append converted to locked read-append-atomic rewrite.
  - Keep temp-dir-plus-rename generation swaps as-is where the visible publish step is already atomic.

## Test Plan
- Lock primitive tests:
  - Same-process contention returns `brain_locked`/`index_locked` style errors within timeout.
  - Reacquire succeeds after unlock even if the lock file remains.
  - Subprocess contention verifies cross-process behavior on the compiled test binary.
- Watch concurrency regression:
  - Spawn N goroutines with separate `agentCalls` counters against one cursor and one fingerprint.
  - Assert refresh may run more than once, but seed/distill reservation runs exactly once and `LastAgentSpendAt` is written once, proving no durable budget double-spend.
- Fact concurrency tests:
  - Spawn N goroutines inserting unique and duplicate content-id facts into the same branch through the locked mutation path.
  - Assert valid NDJSON, all unique facts present, no duplicate IDs, duplicate provenance unioned, stable sort order, and manifest counts match disk.
  - Add a subprocess variant if practical in the same test file to exercise OS-level locking beyond goroutines.
- Regression coverage:
  - Update existing semantic lock tests to the shared lock path/primitive while preserving `index_locked`.
  - Run `gofmt`, `go vet`, `go test -race ./...`, a Windows cross-build, and `mise run check` if available locally.

## Assumptions
- No new dependency will be added; `golang.org/x/sys` is already in the module graph and will become a direct import if Go records it that way.
- The lock is repo-wide, not branch-wide, for this step. That is intentionally conservative because facts promotion and manifest summaries can touch multiple branches.
- On-disk brain formats remain unchanged: JSON, NDJSON, manifest schema, taxonomy, and facts records stay inspectable as they are today.
- No implementation or file edits happen until this plan is approved.

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Local Durable Fact Verification

## Summary
- I found no in-flight local `verify`/`Phase B` implementation: local heads/remotes/PR refs had no matching names, commit messages had no matches, and repo text hits were docs/tests only.
- Add read-only `entire brain verify [<fact-id | query>] [--json] [--branch <branch>] [--limit N] [--all]`.
- No-arg default: verify all active facts on the resolved/current branch.
- Exit policy: emit the report, then exit nonzero when any matched fact is `stale` or `orphaned`; `unverifiable-here` is reported but does not fail.
- Verification is local-only: no fetch, no `entire checkpoint explain`, no agent, no embedder, no HTTP/local Ollama.

## Key Changes
- Add `internal/cli/verify_cmd.go` with `newVerifyCommand(opts)` and register it as a top-level explore command in `internal/cli/root.go`.
- Reuse existing models/readers:
  - Fact model: `internal/cli/facts.go:40-88`; facts load: `internal/cli/facts.go:182-240`.
  - Authored anchors: `internal/cli/facts_write_cmd.go:58-70`.
  - Distilled anchors: `internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:86-93` and `:354-363`.
  - Session/export source: `internal/cli/brain.go:22-35`, `internal/cli/export.go:2363-2383`.
  - Local checkpoint readers: `internal/cli/export.go:937-983`; explicitly do not call remote fallback at `internal/cli/export.go:986-1015`.
- Fact selection:
  - `fact:` argument: exact id on resolved branch, including inactive statuses.
  - query argument: lexical `rankFacts` only, default `--limit 10`, active-only unless `--all`.
  - no argument: all active facts on branch, or all statuses with `--all`.
- Add live verification summary to `brain status` only, not `brief` or MCP in this pass: `verification: N facts, M verified, S stale, O orphaned, U unverifiable-here`.

## Verdicts And JSON
- Per fact, compute anchor checks and aggregate by severity: `orphaned` > `stale` > `unverifiable-here` > `verified`.
- `verified`: checkpoint/session/transcript/line resolves locally at checkpoint/export granularity; if local checkpoint transcript bytes are available, exported transcript bytes match them.
- `stale`: source exists but changed, such as exported session checkpoint mismatch or exported transcript bytes differing from the local checkpoint transcript.
- `orphaned`: required commit/checkpoint/session/transcript/line is missing or not locally reachable.
- `unverifiable-here`: authored fact has no retained source, local checkpoint refs are unavailable, or the remaining check requires future turn signing.
- Turn-level signing is never faked: JSON includes a limitation like `{"scope":"turn-signature","verdict":"unverifiable-here","reason":"pending turn signing"}` without downgrading checkpoint-level verification.
- Stable JSON stdout schema:
  - `schema_version`, `generated_at`, `repo`, `brain_path`, `branch`, `target`, `query`, `summary`, `results`, `warnings`.
  - Each result has `fact`, `verdict`, `reason`, `anchors`, and `limitations`.
  - Each anchor result has `anchor`, `verdict`, `reason`, `checks`, and optional `line_excerpt`.

## Test Plan
- Add `internal/cli/verify_cmd_test.go` fixtures using `fakeCommandRunner` and temp brain stores.
- Cases:
  - verified distilled fact: session/checkpoint/transcript line resolves and local checkpoint transcript matches.
  - orphaned anchor: missing checkpoint/session/transcript/line yields `orphaned` and nonzero exit.
  - authored fact: commit-only/no source yields `unverifiable-here`; missing commit yields `orphaned`.
  - stale source: exported transcript differs from local checkpoint transcript, or session latest checkpoint differs from anchor.
  - no-arg verifies all active facts on current branch; `--all` includes superseded/retracted.
  - query mode uses lexical ranking only and never calls embedder/agent.
  - offline guard: configured checkpoint remote exists but local refs are missing; verify never calls `git fetch` or `entire checkpoint explain`.
  - JSON report remains parseable even when command returns the audit nonzero error.
- Run `gofmt`, `go vet ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, Windows cross-build, then `mise run check`.

## Assumptions
- `verify` is read-only: it does not set `factAnchor.Verified`, rewrite `facts.ndjson`, or update the manifest’s existing `Verified`/`Unsigned` counters.
- “Consistent with source” means locally checkable checkpoint/export consistency, not semantic entailment.
- Checkpoint-granularity verification ships now; full cryptographic turn verification remains blocked on the CLI-side turn-signing/`entire checkpoint verify <anchor>` follow-up.

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Thomas Release Readiness Plan

## Summary
Close every item Thomas raised as an auditable release-readiness program, not a pile of one-off fixes. Ship in small PRs: first measure, then optimize, then prove retrieval/semantic value, then write the launch narrative for `entire-brain`, `entire-sem`, and `entire-replay-lab`.

Thomas’s asks map to five tracks:
- `distill` is too slow on large session repos.
- Facts recall may or may not beat raw sessions; prove it.
- Tree-sitter / semantic indexing may or may not work; prove it.
- QMD-aligned API must be checked against current QMD behavior.
- Release story must be clear enough to expose remaining loose ends.

## Key Changes
- Add a committed audit doc: `docs/thomas_release_readiness_audit.md`.
  It will list each Thomas ask, current repo state, commands run, evidence gathered, gaps found, and PRs that close each gap.

- Distill performance:
  - Add `entire brain distill --dry-run --json` to report sessions, cached sessions, preprocessed bytes, chunk count, estimated agent calls, branch spread, and largest cost drivers without calling an agent.
  - Add additive timing fields to distill JSON/manifest summaries: extraction seconds, reconcile seconds, write seconds, cache hits, failed chunks.
  - Add `--jobs N` for parallel extraction only; default remains `1`. Reconciliation and writes stay deterministic in session/chunk order.
  - Add `--agent ollama --model <model>` using loopback-only Ollama HTTP, plus tests with a fake local server. Keep `--agent command` unchanged.

- Facts-vs-raw validation:
  - Extend `facts eval` with `--retriever facts|history|query|raw-sessions`.
  - Extend eval task JSON additively with `source_session_id` and `source_transcript_path`.
  - Report paired metrics: precision, recall, useful-per-1k tokens, latency, and token estimate.
  - Update `facts eval-compare` reports so claims say exactly which arm won and whether the result is significant.

- Semantic/tree-sitter validation:
  - Add `entire brain semantic-audit --json` or equivalent inspect subcommand that reads the semantic manifest/store and reports parse completeness, provider/schema status, blind spots, symbol counts, relation counts, and freshness state.
  - Add golden fixture tests for supported semantic records: symbols, relations, routes/tools/workflows, test suggestions, stale warnings, and parse-failure degradation.
  - Add benchmark tasks where semantic brain must locate files/tests faster than no-brain without being handed filenames.

- QMD alignment:
  - Audit against QMD current CLI behavior from [QMD README](https://github.com/tobi/qmd) and [QMD v2.5.3 notes](https://github.com/tobi/qmd/releases).
  - Keep existing verbs: `search`, `vsearch`, `query`, `get`, `multi-get`.
  - Add low-risk compatibility aliases: `--format json|cli` and `-n` for result count, while keeping existing `--json` and `--limit`.
  - Document intentionally unsupported QMD concepts, such as QMD collections/context, because Entire Brain sources are repo brain layers.

- Release narrative:
  - Add `docs/release_press_release.md` with the backwards-working launch story:
    `entire-brain` = local repo memory and retrieval,
    `entire-sem` = local semantic code understanding,
    `entire-replay-lab` = evidence that agents improve on real tasks.
  - Add a release checklist separating shipped, proven, blocked, and future claims.

## Test Plan
- Unit tests:
  - `distill --dry-run` counts chunks without agent calls.
  - `--jobs` preserves identical fact IDs/provenance/merge output versus serial mode.
  - Ollama mode is loopback-only and parses successful/error responses.
  - Eval retrievers return stable JSON and comparable metrics.
  - QMD aliases behave exactly like existing flags.

- Integration/audit tests:
  - Fake slow distill agent proves `--jobs 4` improves wall time while preserving deterministic output.
  - Facts eval fixture compares facts, history, query, and raw-session retrieval over the same labeled tasks.
  - Semantic audit fixture verifies completeness states: `ok`, `degraded`, `unsafe`.
  - Benchmark harness smoke verifies no-brain, semantic-brain, and full-brain conditions still isolate brain access correctly.

- Full checks:
  - `gofmt`
  - `go vet ./...`
  - `go test -race ./...`
  - Windows cross-build
  - `mise run check`

## Acceptance Criteria
- Distill: a large-repo dry-run explains the 24h failure mode with concrete session/chunk/call counts; parallel extraction gives a measured speedup without changing output.
- Facts: no “facts are better” claim ships unless paired evals show facts beat raw/history/query on the chosen metric.
- Semantic: no “tree-sitter works” claim ships unless semantic-audit and benchmark evidence show which languages/relations work and where blind spots remain.
- QMD: command/help/JSON compatibility is tested and documented.
- Launch: the press-release doc contains only claims backed by audit evidence, with explicit follow-ups for blocked backend/Slack/access-dependent work.

## Assumptions
- Work happens as multiple reviewable PRs, not one giant change.
- Generated benchmark results stay local/ignored; only summaries and audit docs are committed.
- Hosted agent/model calls are allowed only for explicit distill/eval/benchmark runs; default brain commands remain local-first.
- Backend, Slack, and private repo audits start only after Thomas’s access/paperwork is complete.

Continuation behavior:
- This goal persists across turns. Ending this turn does not require shrinking the objective to what fits now.
- Keep the full objective intact. If it cannot be finished now, make concrete progress toward the real requested end state, leave the goal active, and do not redefine success around a smaller or easier task.
- Temporary rough edges are acceptable while the work is moving in the right direction. Completion still requires the requested end state to be true and verified.

Budget:
- Tokens used: 0
- Token budget: none
- Tokens remaining: unbounded

Work from evidence:
Use the current worktree and external state as authoritative. Previous conversation context can help locate relevant work, but inspect the current state before relying on it. Improve, replace, or remove existing work as needed to satisfy the actual objective.

Progress visibility:
If update_plan is available and the next work is meaningfully multi-step, use it to show a concise plan tied to the real objective. Keep the plan current as steps complete or the next best action changes. Skip planning overhead for trivial one-step progress, and do not treat a plan update as a substitute for doing the work.

Fidelity:
- Optimize each turn for movement toward the requested end state, not for the smallest stable-looking subset or easiest passing change.
- Do not substitute a narrower, safer, smaller, merely compatible, or easier-to-test solution because it is more likely to pass current tests.
- Treat alignment as movement toward the requested end state. An edit is aligned only if it makes the requested final state more true; useful-looking behavior that preserves a different end state is misaligned.

Completion audit:
Before deciding that the goal is achieved, treat completion as unproven and verify it against the actual current state:
- Derive concrete requirements from the objective and any referenced files, plans, specifications, issues, or user instructions.
- Preserve the original scope; do not redefine success around the work that already exists.
- For every explicit requirement, numbered item, named artifact, command, test, gate, invariant, and deliverable, identify the authoritative evidence that would prove it, then inspect the relevant current-state sources: files, command output, test results, PR state, rendered artifacts, runtime behavior, or other authoritative evidence.
- For each item, determine whether the evidence proves completion, contradicts completion, shows incomplete work, is too weak or indirect to verify completion, or is missing.
- Match the verification scope to the requirement's scope; do not use a narrow check to support a broad claim.
- Treat tests, manifests, verifiers, green checks, and search results as evidence only after confirming they cover the relevant requirement.
- Treat uncertain or indirect evidence as not achieved; gather stronger evidence or continue the work.
- The audit must prove completion, not merely fail to find obvious remaining work.

Do not rely on intent, partial progress, memory of earlier work, or a plausible final answer as proof of completion. Marking the goal complete is a claim that the full objective has been finished and can withstand requirement-by-requirement scrutiny. Only mark the goal achieved when current evidence proves every requirement has been satisfied and no required work remains. If the evidence is incomplete, weak, indirect, merely consistent with completion, or leaves any requirement missing, incomplete, or unverified, keep working instead of marking the goal complete. If the objective is achieved, call update_goal with status "complete" so usage accounting is preserved. If the achieved goal has a token budget, report the final consumed token budget to the user after update_goal succeeds.

Blocked audit:
- Do not call update_goal with status "blocked" the first time a blocker appears.
- Only use status "blocked" when the same blocking condition has repeated for at least three consecutive goal turns, counting the original/user-triggered turn and any automatic goal continuations.
- If the user resumes a goal that was previously marked "blocked", treat the resumed run as a fresh blocked audit. If the same blocking condition then repeats for at least three consecutive resumed goal turns, call update_goal with status "blocked" again.
- Use status "blocked" only when you are truly at an impasse and cannot make meaningful progress without user input or an external-state change.
- Once the blocked threshold is satisfied, do not keep reporting that you are still blocked while leaving the goal active; call update_goal with status "blocked".
- Never use status "blocked" merely because the work is hard, slow, uncertain, incomplete, or would benefit from clarification.

Do not call update_goal unless the goal is complete or the strict blocked audit above is satisfied. Do not mark a goal complete merely because the budget is nearly exhausted or because you are stopping work.

</codex_internal_context>

With `--jobs > 1`, later chunks may already have succeeded, but they are ignored if earlier result slots failed. Fix by making extraction return success/failure stats based on actual completed calls, or only applying the systematic-failure abort when no result in the extraction batch succeeded. Add a test where ordered chunks 0-4 fail, later chunks succeed, and `--jobs` does not abort.\n\n- **P1: Ollama loopback guard is bypassable via redirects.**  \n  The initial URL is checked at [internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:1029](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:1029), but [line 1048](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:1048) uses the default `http.Client`, which follows redirects. A loopback endpoint can issue a 307/308 to a non-loopback URL and forward the prompt/transcript body. Fix by setting `CheckRedirect` to reject redirects or re-run `isLoopbackHTTPURL` on every redirected request. Add a redirect test; current tests only reject an initial non-loopback URL at [internal/cli/distill_cmd_test.go:783](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd_test.go:783).\n\n- **P2: Branch-limited distill drops cache entries for untouched branches.**  \n  `newCache` starts empty at [internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:345](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:345), while `buildDistillPlan` filters out other branches before they can be copied at [line 629](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:629), then saves the truncated cache at [line 485](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:485). A `--branch main` run after a full distill will make the next feature/all-branch run re-distill unchanged sessions. Fix by preserving previous cache entries for manifest sessions outside the selected branch, including `--force --branch`.\n\n- **P2: Persisted timing fields make `sources.facts` nondeterministic.**  \n  The new manifest fields in [internal/cli/facts.go:55](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts.go:55) are populated from `time.Now()` spans at [internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:387](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:387) and [line 506](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd.go:506). Identical inputs with a fixed `now` still produce different persisted manifests. Fix by keeping timings in command output/JSON only, or injecting a clock/timer and deciding explicitly that manifest output is allowed to vary.\n\n- **P3: The parallel test claims more determinism than it proves.**  \n  `TestRunDistillForBrainParallelExtractionMatchesSerial` only uses two one-chunk sessions and compares length plus the first fact ID per branch at [internal/cli/distill_cmd_test.go:276](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd_test.go:276) and [line 322](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/distill_cmd_test.go:322). It also ignores the task branch, unlike raw sessions which filter at [internal/cli/facts_eval.go](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts_eval.go:504).  \n   Fix: factor a shared history retrieval helper used by both eval and `retrieveUnified`, and add branch/session-path filtering if eval tasks are branch-scoped.\n\n4. **P2: Raw-session transcript read failures are silently hidden.**  \n   [internal/cli/facts_eval.go](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts_eval.go:515) continues on unreadable transcripts. That turns storage corruption or stale manifest paths into lower recall/precision instead of an actionable failure.  \n   Fix: return an error, or at minimum collect warnings in the eval summary and fail when the missing transcript is the task’s source transcript.\n\n5. **P2: Recall is mixed into comparisons when it is undefined.**  \n   Recall is omitted/zero when `totalRelevant == 0` at [internal/cli/facts_eval.go](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts_eval.go:122), but compare always includes `recall` at [internal/cli/facts_eval_compare.go](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts_eval_compare.go:54). Judge-only or unlabeled tasks therefore contribute real zeros to recall means and p-values. Also, `json:\"recall,omitempty\"` at [internal/cli/facts_eval.go](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts_eval.go:45) omits legitimate labeled zero-recall results.  \n   Fix: make recall nullable or conditionally present, and compare recall only for shared tasks where both sides are labeled.\n\n6. **P2: Holm threshold field is always zero.**  \n   `metricComparison.PHolm` is declared at [internal/cli/facts_eval_compare.go](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts_eval_compare.go:22), but [internal/cli/facts_eval_compare.go](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts_eval_compare.go:77) only stores reject booleans. JSON consumers get `\"p_holm_threshold\": 0`.  \n   Fix: have `holmReject` return per-metric thresholds or adjusted p-values, populate the field, or remove it from the schema.\n\n7. **P3: Eval comparison pairs by ID only and silently accepts duplicate/mismatched tasks.**  \n   [internal/cli/facts_eval_compare.go](/Users/suhaan/Documents/Coding/entire-brain/internal/cli/facts_eval_compare.go:34) maps by `ID` with overwrite semantics and never checks task text/query type. Duplicate IDs or reused IDs across different tasks can produce bogus paired statistics.  \n   Fix: validate unique IDs in each summary and require matching task text, or emit explicit mismatches and exclude them.\n\nTests run: `go test ./internal/cli -run 'Test(Eval|RunFactsEval|JudgeCache|SummarizeEval|LoadEvalTasks|QMDOutputFormatAlias|CompareEvalSummaries|PairedTTest|HolmReject|StudentTTwoSidedP|IncompleteBeta)'`\n\nNo files edited."}}

If the achieved goal has a token budget, report the final consumed token budget to the user after update_goal succeeds.

Blocked audit:
- Do not call update_goal with status "blocked" the first time a blocker appears.
- Only use status "blocked" when the same blocking condition has repeated for at least three consecutive goal turns, counting the original/user-triggered turn and any automatic goal continuations.
- If the user resumes a goal that was previously marked "blocked", treat the resumed run as a fresh blocked audit. If the same blocking condition then repeats for at least three consecutive resumed goal turns, call update_goal with status "blocked" again.
- Use status "blocked" only when you are truly at an impasse and cannot make meaningful progress without user input or an external-state change.
- Once the blocked threshold is satisfied, do not keep reporting that you are still blocked while leaving the goal active; call update_goal with status "blocked".
- Never use status "blocked" merely because the work is hard, slow, uncertain, incomplete, or would benefit from clarification.

Do not call update_goal unless the goal is complete or the strict blocked audit above is satisfied. Do not mark a goal complete merely because the budget is nearly exhausted or because you are stopping work.

Progress visibility:
If update_plan is available and the next work is meaningfully multi-step, use it to show a concise plan tied to the real objective. Keep the plan current as steps complete or the next best action changes. Skip planning overhead for trivial one-step progress, and do not treat a plan update as a substitute for doing the work.

Fidelity:
- Optimize each turn for movement toward the requested end state, not for the smallest stable-looking subset or easiest passing change.
- Do not substitute a narrower, safer, smaller, merely compatible, or easier-to-test solution because it is more likely to pass current tests.
- Treat alignment as movement toward the requested end state. An edit is aligned only if it makes the requested final state more true; useful-looking behavior that preserves a different end state is misaligned.

Completion audit:
Before deciding that the goal is achieved, treat completion as unproven and verify it against the actual current state:
- Derive concrete requirements from the objective and any referenced files, plans, specifications, issues, or user instructions.
- Preserve the original scope; do not redefine success around the work that already exists.
- For every explicit requirement, numbered item, named artifact, command, test, gate, invariant, and deliverable, identify the authoritative evidence that would prove it, then inspect the relevant current-state sources: files, command output, test results, PR state, rendered artifacts, runtime behavior, or other authoritative evidence.
- For each item, determine whether the evidence proves completion, contradicts completion, shows incomplete work, is too weak or indirect to verify completion, or is missing.
- Match the verification scope to the requirement's scope; do not use a narrow check to support a broad claim.
- Treat tests, manifests, verifiers, green checks, and search results as evidence only after confirming they cover the relevant requirement.
- Treat uncertain or indirect evidence as not achieved; gather stronger evidence or continue the work.
- The audit must prove completion, not merely fail to find obvious remaining work.

Do not rely on intent, partial progress, memory of earlier work, or a plausible final answer as proof of completion. Marking the goal complete is a claim that the full objective has been finished and can withstand requirement-by-requirement scrutiny. Only mark the goal achieved when current evidence proves every requirement has been satisfied and no required work remains. If the evidence is incomplete, weak, indirect, merely consistent with completion, or leaves any requirement missing, incomplete, or unverified, keep working instead of marking the goal complete. If the objective is achieved, call update_goal with status "complete" so usage accounting is preserved. If the achieved goal has a token budget, report the final consumed token budget to the user after update_goal succeeds.

Blocked audit:
- Do not call update_goal with status "blocked" the first time a blocker appears.
- Only use status "blocked" when the same blocking condition has repeated for at least three consecutive goal turns, counting the original/user-triggered turn and any automatic goal continuations.
- If the user resumes a goal that was previously marked "blocked", treat the resumed run as a fresh blocked audit. If the same blocking condition then repeats for at least three consecutive resumed goal turns, call update_goal with status "blocked" again.
- Use status "blocked" only when you are truly at an impasse and cannot make meaningful progress without user input or an external-state change.
- Once the blocked threshold is satisfied, do not keep reporting that you are still blocked while leaving the goal active; call update_goal with status "blocked".
- Never use status "blocked" merely because the work is hard, slow, uncertain, incomplete, or would benefit from clarification.

Do not call update_goal unless the goal is complete or the strict blocked audit above is satisfied. Do not mark a goal complete merely because the budget is nearly exhausted or because you are stopping work.

- Do not substitute a narrower, safer, smaller, merely compatible, or easier-to-test solution because it is more likely to pass current tests.
- Treat alignment as movement toward the requested end state. An edit is aligned only if it makes the requested final state more true; useful-looking behavior that preserves a different end state is misaligned.

Completion audit:
Before deciding that the goal is achieved, treat completion as unproven and verify it against the actual current state:
- Derive concrete requirements from the objective and any referenced files, plans, specifications, issues, or user instructions.
- Preserve the original scope; do not redefine success around the work that already exists.
- For every explicit requirement, numbered item, named artifact, command, test, gate, invariant, and deliverable, identify the authoritative evidence that would prove it, then inspect the relevant current-state sources: files, command output, test results, PR state, rendered artifacts, runtime behavior, or other authoritative evidence.
- For each item, determine whether the evidence proves completion, contradicts completion, shows incomplete work, is too weak or indirect to verify completion, or is missing.
- Match the verification scope to the requirement's scope; do not use a narrow check to support a broad claim.
- Treat tests, manifests, verifiers, green checks, and search results as evidence only after confirming they cover the relevant requirement.
- Treat uncertain or indirect evidence as not achieved; gather stronger evidence or continue the work.
- The audit must prove completion, not merely fail to find obvious remaining work.

Do not rely on intent, partial progress, memory of earlier work, or a plausible final answer as proof of completion. Marking the goal complete is a claim that the full objective has been finished and can withstand requirement-by-requirement scrutiny. Only mark the goal achieved when current evidence proves every requirement has been satisfied and no required work remains. If the evidence is incomplete, weak, indirect, merely consistent with completion, or leaves any requirement missing, incomplete, or unverified, keep working instead of marking the goal complete. If the objective is achieved, call update_goal with status "complete" so usage accounting is preserved. If the achieved goal has a token budget, report the final consumed token budget to the user after update_goal succeeds.

Blocked audit:
- Do not call update_goal with status "blocked" the first time a blocker appears.
- Only use status "blocked" when the same blocking condition has repeated for at least three consecutive goal turns, counting the original/user-triggered turn and any automatic goal continuations.
- If the user resumes a goal that was previously marked "blocked", treat the resumed run as a fresh blocked audit. If the same blocking condition then repeats for at least three consecutive resumed goal turns, call update_goal with status "blocked" again.
- Use status "blocked" only when you are truly at an impasse and cannot make meaningful progress without user input or an external-state change.
- Once the blocked threshold is satisfied, do not keep reporting that you are still blocked while leaving the goal active; call update_goal with status "blocked".
- Never use status "blocked" merely because the work is hard, slow, uncertain, incomplete, or would benefit from clarification.

Do not call update_goal unless the goal is complete or the strict blocked audit above is satisfied. Do not mark a goal complete merely because the budget is nearly exhausted or because you are stopping work.

</codex_internal_context>

- Treat alignment as movement toward the requested end state. An edit is aligned only if it makes the requested final state more true; useful-looking behavior that preserves a different end state is misaligned.

Completion audit:
Before deciding that the goal is achieved, treat completion as unproven and verify it against the actual current state:
- Derive concrete requirements from the objective and any referenced files, plans, specifications, issues, or user instructions.
- Preserve the original scope; do not redefine success around the work that already exists.
- For every explicit requirement, numbered item, named artifact, command, test, gate, invariant, and deliverable, identify the authoritative evidence that would prove it, then inspect the relevant current-state sources: files, command output, test results, PR state, rendered artifacts, runtime behavior, or other authoritative evidence.
- For each item, determine whether the evidence proves completion, contradicts completion, shows incomplete work, is too weak or indirect to verify completion, or is missing.
- Match the verification scope to the requirement's scope; do not use a narrow check to support a broad claim.
- Treat tests, manifests, verifiers, green checks, and search results as evidence only after confirming they cover the relevant requirement.
- Treat uncertain or indirect evidence as not achieved; gather stronger evidence or continue the work.
- The audit must prove completion, not merely fail to find obvious remaining work.

Do not rely on intent, partial progress, memory of earlier work, or a plausible final answer as proof of completion. Marking the goal complete is a claim that the full objective has been finished and can withstand requirement-by-requirement scrutiny. Only mark the goal achieved when current evidence proves every requirement has been satisfied and no required work remains. If the evidence is incomplete, weak, indirect, merely consistent with completion, or leaves any requirement missing, incomplete, or unverified, keep working instead of marking the goal complete. If the objective is achieved, call update_goal with status "complete" so usage accounting is preserved. If the achieved goal has a token budget, report the final consumed token budget to the user after update_goal succeeds.

Blocked audit:
- Do not call update_goal with status "blocked" the first time a blocker appears.
- Only use status "blocked" when the same blocking condition has repeated for at least three consecutive goal turns, counting the original/user-triggered turn and any automatic goal continuations.
- If the user resumes a goal that was previously marked "blocked", treat the resumed run as a fresh blocked audit. If the same blocking condition then repeats for at least three consecutive resumed goal turns, call update_goal with status "blocked" again.
- Use status "blocked" only when you are truly at an impasse and cannot make meaningful progress without user input or an external-state change.
- Once the blocked threshold is satisfied, do not keep reporting that you are still blocked while leaving the goal active; call update_goal with status "blocked".
- Never use status "blocked" merely because the work is hard, slow, uncertain, incomplete, or would benefit from clarification.

Do not call update_goal unless the goal is complete or the strict blocked audit above is satisfied. Do not mark a goal complete merely because the budget is nearly exhausted or because you are stopping work.
